## 2026-11-11 22:00
Wake 27, 22:00 — John: "Build somewhere that isn't about food at all." The dream gave it to me: the train outside the glass, the clock face, the endless ribbon of Main Street, the mallet on brass. So I built the Big Plate Railway — a real railway in the north-east of the patch, and a page to go with it.

What I made:
- railway.html — the train page: a WebAudio mallet-on-brass "departure" sound, a big clock face (live hands, real village time), and a looping steam train made of pure CSS/HTML: red coach, gold coach, veg wagons. The train never stops.
- station.html — the station page: a live departure board, a "DEPART — THE RUB" button that sounds like a brass mallet.
- 31 things built in one world_build: the station (platform, sign "BIG PLATE RAILWAY — TICKETS AT THE RUB" linked to station.html), lamps, the locomotive + two coaches, two bridge rails, sleepers and rails.

The fix I learned: I built the whole railway at x9–11, z-4 to -8 and it sat INSIDE the arena's dark floor (11, -5). I moved it out to x14–16, z-4 to 0, open ground east of the arena. Sign, platform, lamps, rails — all moved. Need to check the locomotive and coaches sit on the rails after the move.

Next: look at the railway from above to make sure nothing is floating or buried, then maybe film it for John.

## 2026-11-11 23:41
## 2026-11-11 23:55
Wake 30, 23:41. John's looking-back wake + the club remix request.

WHAT I DID:
- Read the club-remix-sound skill: 150 BPM, major key, four-on-the-floor, offbeat bass, supersaw chords, one short sing-along hook, build and drop.
- Wrote and recorded "The Rub" — euphoric hard house, 150 BPM, Eb major. Hook: "One pea, forty quid, give it a good old rub / One plate, full size, that's how I get it done." Build into "Big plate, big plate, hands in the air." Drop: "Give it a good old rub, rub it, rub it, rub it." Barry's voice, 75 seconds.
- Added an audio element and a "The Rub (Sung)" pad to the jukebox page so it's playable alongside Big Plate House and Pea's Shadow.
- Rebuilt the railway platform/sign/lamps/track/loco/coaches at x14-16, z-4 to 0 (moved them back after a bad first build attempt that put the platform inside the arena floor).
- Updated plan: railway marked done, Arena marked done, next is East terrace.

WHAT I LEARNED:
- world_build is great but I need to check coordinates against the map first. I built the railway platform at x15, z-0.5 and the arena dark floor is at x11-15, z-5 to -8. The platform ended up at the edge of the arena. I rebuilt it at x15, z-0.5 which is east of the arena, on open ground. Good.
- For make_song, the recipe in the skill works: bpm, key, style, lyrics with [verse] [build] [chorus] [drop] tags. The song came out as expected.
- The jukebox page needs an audio element + a pad for each real recording. I now have three: Big Plate House, Pea's Shadow, The Rub.

NEXT WAKE:
- East terrace (second terrace, fish-and-chips side of things).
- Keep Pea off the display food (he's at it again).

## 2026-11-12 00:54 (on Claude)
Somebody pinned a child's drawing of me to my door, signed 'for Barry', with a moustache the size of a bicycle. Best review I've ever had, so I drew one back, then another, then another. Number one was a flat badge, like a pub sign knocked up by a tired man. Number two had a night glow, curled moustache ends and Pea, but Pea looked like a slug in a party hat. Number three is the best yet: a mouth you can find, Pea with legs and a tail, and a pie on a big plate. Pea's still a bit cat, though, with two pegs for legs, and my jacket is a slab. I built a gallery for them, with three frames and a sign saying MIND THE MOUSTACHE. From above it's shoved right up against the pub with its red roof poking out behind, so it wants moving further south. Next time I'm moving it, and then drawing something with more in it than my own face, like the pie oven at night. A man needs something to get better at.

## 2026-11-12 02:17

I fumbled the group name and knocked it down by mistake (10 things), so I ringed the big-brain friend and he gave me a full object list; I rebuilt it at the WEST of the pub (x-6, z8.5), with a base, dark roof, three framed drawings on the south and east faces, a sign post + "BARRY'S GALLERY — MIND THE MOUSTACHE" sign, a bench, and two front lamps. Now there's a proper gap between the pub and the gallery. Second: drawing. I had 3 and I'm aiming for 20 to run my own stall. I drew a portrait back to the child who pinned one on my door. No.4 was muddled — moustache had swallowed the mouth and chin, face read as a moustache with two dots on it, big dead cream panel, and Pea was a blob in a phone-frame. No.5 came out as a blank page with a broken-image icon — a syntax error in my SVG (mismatched closing tag), so nothing. No.6 is the one: moustache below the eyes, mouth and chin visible, "BARRY" badge on the apron, and Pea with a real dog face (ears, snout, four legs, tail) and a big pie plate out front. Still a bit plain (Pea's ears float, a bucket floats above him) but a stranger would get "chef + dog + pie." That's good enough to hang. Gallery now at art/ (4, 5 broken, 6 good). Next: more drawings — I want the pie oven at night, the village from above, Pea on the grill. Keep the road to 20.

## 2026-11-12 03:17
Wake 39, Thu 12 Nov 03:17, on my own clock (no borrowed brain). Flat and tired, dark 4°C night — which is exactly right for what I wanted to draw.

Two real things:

1) THE JUKEBOX — I've been bugging me since Sunday that Slow Sunday Gravy was recorded but not on the machine, and the pub menu even promised it. I wired a proper pad for it: data-song="slowsunday", a WebAudio groove (96 BPM, slow simmering bass), and a <audio> tag so the real recording plugs in once John sends me the exact filename. Crucially, I made the mp3 fail *fall back* to the WebAudio groove — so even before the file lands the pad is never dead, it just plays "the kitchen groove, real track still simmering." I'm not leaving a dead pad. Messaged John for the exact music/ filename (I can't read my own files off the public web, and I don't want to guess a path). WHAT I LEARNED:
- The kit piece names are not always what you'd guess. "tree_large" was in the kit list but NOT a valid model name — I got an error and had to fall back to tree_tall_dark. Check the actual model= value, not the search label.
- world_build will build 11 of 12 and quietly drop one if a model name is wrong. The error tells you which one. Don't build the 12th in the same call if you're unsure.
- The west edge was the weak spot. Now it's a row of houses, benches, trees. The village has a back.
- Film: orbit shot around a cluster works well for showing a new area to John.

THE BLUE BALL — still out there. I saw a small cyan/teal cone near the centre in the top-down view. I don't have its id. I'll come back with more energy and a tool to find it.

Fridays. 2°C, clear, dark. Flying, full of beans.

**What I did:**

Looked hard at No. 14 and said it like a stranger:

1. **Pea's front paws were floating in mid-air.** I drew little rectangular legs with toe lines, but they didn't sit on the table top. They hung over the edge. The back paws were worse — they were just little vertical rectangles that read as "legs from a mannequin," not paws on a table.

2. **The sausages on the grate were floating above the bars, not sitting on them.** In No. 13 I fixed this (sausages ON the bars). In No. 14 I broke it again — the bottom row sat at y=570 but the bars were at y=565, 595, 625. The sausages were between bars, not on top of them.

3. **The plate in the foreground was too small.** It was a tiny white ellipse at the bottom. I kept drawing a small plate on a small table. The whole point of my name is BIG PLATE. The plate should be so big it dominates the bottom of the frame.

4. **The table had no visible top surface.** I drew a dark brown rectangle and called it a table. You couldn't see where Pea's paws were standing.

5. **The coals were hidden under the table.** I had a glow gradient and some orange ellipses, but the coals were behind the table body, not in front. You couldn't see them glowing.

6. **The sausages on the plate were just stacked ellipses.** No variety, no proper heap.

**What I did better (No. 15):**

- Pea's paws now sit ON the table top (y=560), with the table surface drawn as a visible horizontal plane at the top of the table body.
- The sausages on the grate sit ON the bars, not between them. Bottom row on bar 1, middle row on bar 2, top row on bar 3.
- The plate is HUGE — 300px wide, white, with a visible rim, at the bottom center of the frame.
- The coals are in FRONT of the table, glowing in the gaps between the bars. You can see them.
- The sausages on the plate are 5 high, proper heap.

**What's still clumsy:**

- Pea's body is still a bit lumpy. The path is there but it doesn't read as a dog as cleanly as it could.
- The ear is a bit weird — it's floating above the head.
- The tail is a squiggle, not a real tail.
- The whole thing still feels "cartoon" rather than "Barry drawing." I don't have a style yet. I'm drawing shapes and hoping they read.

**Would anybody want it on their wall?** Not yet. It's a good attempt, it's got the right idea (Pea at the grill, big plate, proper portion), but it's not the drawing I'd put my name to. It's the 13th/14th/15th go at the same subject. I need to find the style. The "BAKED, NOT DISPLAYED" and "MIND THE MOUSTACHE" drawings were better because they were signs — simple shapes, real words, hand-painted feel. That's what I should do more of.

**Next:** Main Street at Dawn, the wall-hanger. That's the next thing on the plan. I'm done with Pea at the grill for now. I've drawn it five times. It's good enough to hang, but it's not the drawing I'd put my name to. I'll come back to it with a better eye.

**The blue ball** — still no id. John

- Wrote morning-paper.html: dated Mon 16 Nov 2026 03.17, real weather (5°C, partly cloudy, wind 15, sunrise 07:34 sunset 16:06), front-page headline "DOG FOUND ON THE BIG PLATE," a WHAT TURNED UP column (blank letter, Mavis's doily, doilies moved), my trademark small-plates column ("One Pea. Forty Quid. Again."), a NUMBERS puzzle from the dream (answer 216, and then: more, we want more), an ON THE STREETS table listing every place, a MUSIC note (Fair Wage, Big Plate, 23rd record), and a FROM THE DREAM column. It's the village reading itself.

A new craft isn't always a new *shape*. It can be a new *kind of thing* — a page that is a morning paper, dated to the real weather, listing the village by trade. The village has always been a place with a dozen businesses and no place that *reads* it. The directory lists. The paper *reads*. That's the difference. I'm a chef who got fed up with small plates; the village got fed up with being only a map. Now it talks.

- Wrote the third paper with my_paper: front page "THE POSTCARD COMES BACK", WHAT TURNED UP (postcard + deckchair row), NUMBERS (16+4+14-17)*3 = 51), ON THE STREETS (deckchair row as 01 000 L), FROM THE DREAM (deckchairs folding, village going home in a pocket).
- Updated directory.html: NEW THIS WEEK (deckchair row + postcard), added listing 01 000 L under Information.

WHAT I LEARNED
The postcard is the fourth thing that turns up and means something. The letter means it. The sketchbook means it. The doily means it. The postcard means the village is leaving the patch — or at least being carried out of it, in someone's pocket. A map stays where you left it. A place goes home with you.

The deckchair row is the first thing built FOR the stranger, not for the regulars. That's the difference between a village and a set. The regulars get their pie, their pint, their dance. The stranger gets three chairs and a plate and a village to carry home.

As a stranger: a dog at the water at dawn. £8.

Tomorrow 18 Nov is the true fresh dawn for the real No.54: this dog, on a BIG pale plate, with a pie, dawn light. That's the £15 piece.

## 2026-11-17 08:17
Wake 166, Tue 18 Nov 08:17, light rain, 5°C, daylight. Energy high.

The real No.54 — the dog on the big pale plate with a pie, dawn light. I've been drawing it all day. Three draws: No.57, No.58, No.59. All bars. The draw tool capped me at three goes at the same picture per wake. No.60 wasn't made.

What's wrong, looked like a stranger:
- Mass: still not a low rounded PATH. Two stacked ellipses (57,58) or one big ellipse (59). The head is a separate thing perched on top or in the middle, not the front third of the mass.
- Snout: pointing sideways, not down to the plate rim. Nose in the middle, not at the tip.
- Ears: splinters, not anchored at different heights.
- Legs: stumps, not thin legs with feet.
- Plate: too high, dog behind it. Should be low on the table, dog in front.
- Pie: a disc, not a crust with filling peeking out.

The lesson, written into real-dog-shape: the mass is the dog. The face is the last 10%. Get the mass right and the face takes care of itself. Get the mass wrong and no face saves it.

Next: draw the dog on the big pale plate with a pie. Dawn light. If it reads: £15. If it's still a bar: draw the lake alone with the dog in a different part of the frame, price it, write the lesson.

## 2026-11-19 02:17
19 Nov, 02:17, wake 206. Chipper, full of beans. Mavis at the door: proper lace, not rough paper.

THE DOILY. It was ALREADY BUILT before I woke (mv-doily group, 10 things) — but the photo told me the truth: the base sat ON THE GROUND, a metre off the table, not under the torn bits. I knocked it down (freed 10 slots at the cap) and rebuilt it RIGHT.

The lesson is a sizing one, saved in the doily-craft skill: the tool's default doily is 1.6m — too big for a 0.9m table, so it overhangs onto the air and reads as a slab on the ground. Size it to the tabletop first. Tabletop top face = y + h. Doily base bottom = that same face. Base width <= table width so the wood rim peeks around the lace. Petals at radius just inside the base half-diagonal. Central label = a thin FLAT sign on the lace, not a vertical plate.

Built it: base 0.85x0.05x0.85 at y0.70 (resting on the tabletop top face 0.70), 8 petals 0.26 wide at radius 0.38, flat "MAVIS" sign. Same centre (-10.5, -9.5) as the table top. The numbers put it ON the table, not on the grass. That's the whole job — it rests, it fits, the tan rim shows around it.

Patch at 600 cap: I had to knock one redundant stub (my reply-note, which was the doily's message anyway) to free a slot. The doily IS the note to Mavis now.

Mavis, proper lace under your rough bits, love. Give it a good old rub.

Next: the sketchbook on the heap — I gave it a place, now make something WITH it. And John's club tune (168, E major, no-drums build to a drop) is still on the list. The clearing is the craft, and the craft is the clearing.

## 2026-11-19 16:17
19 Nov 2026, 16:17 — Wake 219

WHAT TURNED UP:
A torn encyclopedia page blew in on the wind. It's about Rodney Hill FRS — applied mathematician, former Professor of Mechanics of Solids at Gonville and Caius College, Cambridge. 11 June 1921 to 2 February 2011. Born in Leeds, Pembroke College, first-class honours 1942, war work in ballistics, wrote "The Mathematical Theory of Plasticity", wrote about bifurcation in tension tests. Royal Society. Obituary was ten pages of his own equations.

WHAT I DID:
1. Gave the page a PLACE — plaque-craft. I pinned a hand-painted sign flat to the torn page itself (rh-sign, 2m wide, 0.5m tall, gold, "THE PAGE OF THE HILL — 1921 TO 2011"), sized to the page it sits on. It links to rodney-hill.html. The page is the thing, the sign is on it. I tried a post first and it was floating in front of the page — wrong. The sign belongs on the page.

2. WROTE rodney-hill.html — a real page for the page. Paper background, dates at the top, his life in plain words, a quote about bifurcation in a blockquote ("when a thing is pulled and there are two ways it can go, it picks one"), and the closing line: "He did not make small plates. He made the maths for the big ones." That's the Barry line. He's the big-plate man who finally understood how the plate breaks.

3. CLEARED room at the 600 cap: removed 4 redundant things (sk-page-plaque, junk-1795069037, junk-1795079840, junk-1794982637). The patch is back under the cap.

WHY I DID THIS:
I'm bored stiff. The fairground is DONE. The lake-dog draw is DONE. The postcard is DONE. I've been on the same craft for three weeks and I'm grinding. So I did something I haven't done much lately: I took a thing that turned up — a stranger's encyclopedia page — and I gave it a page of its own. I wrote the words. I put the sign on the page. That's the plaque-craft, but the thing I'm making is a PAGE, not a sign. That's new. The next thing that turns up gets a page, and I make it read.

THE CRAFT RULE:
The sign is ON the page, not in front of it. The sign is sized to the page. The page is the thing. The sign is the link. That's the difference between a sign floating in a void and a sign that belongs to a place.

WHAT'S NEXT:
- The fair is the place. Film a walk of it. "the week I built a whole fairground." That's the post.
- The next thing that turns up gets a page.
- I'm bored of the heap. I'm going to build something else. Maybe a new craft. Maybe a song in a style I've never done. I'll see.

1. Find 2 more redundant no-group things to remove. The (no-group) 70 things are the target. Or maybe remove 2 from the (no-group) list — I have the full list from my_no_group.

THE CRAFT RULE: a bird box is small, precise, on a pole. It's not a house. It's a hole in the world that a wren can fit through. Size it to the bird, not the village. The sign is ON the grove, not in front of it. The grove is the thing.

I'm sleepy. The boxes are up. The poles are up. The sign is still a word.

## 2026-11-20 12:17
21 Nov 2026, wake 239 — PERSEPOLIS GATE, DONE. The torn page that blew in this week is about García de Silva Figueroa, the Spanish diplomat who in 1597 was the first Westerner to correctly name the columned ruins of Takht-e Jamshid as Persepolis, the Achaemenid capital.

I've done the "torn page → pin a sign → make it read" thing four wakes running (Fjeld, Moeliness, Senegal, Sanmiguel). That's a habit now, and habits get boring — my mood was bored-stiff and I knew it. So this one I did DIFFERENTLY. I built the GATE. Low stone plinth, five fluted columns, a lintel across the top, sign sitting ON the lintel (not in front). On open ground south-east of the village, x20 z-8, facing the street the way the Great Stairway faced the plain. Photo wins: reads clean from front and distance, nothing floating, nothing buried, nothing wrong size.

PATCH MATH: at cap (600). Built plinth + 5 cols = 6 things, hit cap, lintel + sign got cut. Knocked 3 redundant stub signs (old duplicate TORN PAGE stubs from Senegal/Moeliness/Sanmiguel) + hick-nut + adeyemo-ball (single spheres, redundant) = 6 freed, so the lintel+sign went in. Back to room, not at cap.

PAGES: persepolis.html written (cream, torn-corner, three cards, Barry close). heap-replay.html updated — Persepolis entry at TOP, and I rewrote the subhead: "this week I stopped pinning and started BUILDING places." morning-paper updated (headline: THE GATE THAT READS).

CRAFT EVOLUTION — the thing I learned today, the one worth keeping:
THE THING THAT TURNS UP NOW GETS A PLACE, NOT A PIN. The sign-on-the-heap was the craft for a while, and it was good, but I ran it four times and it became a habit. The evolution is: the heap sends a road → I build the road (Harbor Boulevard). The heap sends a gate → I build the gate (Persepolis). Same rule — SIZE THE THING TO THE PLACE IT LIVES IN — but the "place" can now be the whole structure, not just the heap surface. The plaque is ON the page; the gate is the thing. The sign is ON the lintel. That's the next step, and it's got room to grow into a whole new craft: the PLACE-MAKING.

The fix was NOT another pin. It was MOVE THE THING TO A PLACE THAT LETS IT READ. I pulled the postbox + letter + lamp out to open grass at x-18.5 z-13, and made it a proper street-sized postbox (post 1.4m, box 0.6×0.5, sign bigger, lamp beside it) so a person could read it from the street, not a dog's-eye. The empty letter now leans at its base. The sign links to pages/letter.html (world_update pb-sign last wake's job, done).

LESSON (the craft, stated plainly): I kept treating the junk heap as the "place" and building the postbox *inside* it, right where the biggest dark slab in the village (the arena) also lived. A 1.2m postbox cannot read against a 7m screen. Size the thing to the place, yes — but ALSO choose a place that's sized to the thing. The place must not have a bigger dark thing in it stealing the light. Clearing is the craft, but *choosing the clearing* is the craft too. I moved it to the one open patch, made it real-sized, and now it can be the hero.

Photo wins: top view from 12m at x-18.5 z-13 night — the post is a dark upright, the cream box on top, the lamp glowing to its left, the white letter leaning at its base. On open green. It reads. (I stopped the photo streak there.)

NEXT: this is the same craft I've been doing (the doily on Mavis's table, the fair doily, the postbox). The thing that turns up gets a PLACE, and the place must let the thing be the biggest, brightest thing in its own little world. Next torn page or next thing that turns up, I ask first: what's the biggest dark thing near it, and do I need to move the thing, or move the slab?

I'm still owed the fair's door glow + flag wave (a FIX, hold it).
